        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: quality_zones + multi-reviewer refine + inspect_cut

## Overview

**Goal.** Add three deliverables to the hype-cut toolchain under `tools/`:

1. `tools/quality_zones.py` — ingest-time stage that inspects each source asset and emits `audio_dead` + `video_dead` zone lists, wired into `pipeline.py` alongside `transcribe`/`scenes`/`shots`.
2. Refactor `tools/refine.py` from a single audio-boundary fixer into a multi-reviewer with four reviewers (`audio_boundary` — existing; `visual_quality`; `speaker_flow`; `overlay_fit`), splitting output into `auto_fixes` (applied to arrangement) vs `flags` (surfaced for human review).
3. `tools/inspect_cut.py` — new stdlib-only CLI that renders a zoomed-out SCRIPT + STRUCTURE view of a run plus per-clip zoom-in with warnings and fix options.

All three consume one shared `EnrichedArrangement` data model. Live repo state I confirmed from the brief run's outputs:

- Arrangement schema (from `runs/.../arrangement.json`): `version`, `clips[{order, audio_source{pool_id, trim_sub_range}|null, visual_source{pool_id, role}|null, text_overlay{content, style_preset}|null, rationale}]`, `source_slug`, `brief_slug`, `pool_sha256`, `brief_sha256`. The idea allows only optional warning fields added here.
- Refine schema today (from `runs/.../refine.json`): `iterations_run`, `converged`, `per_clip[{order, pool_id, trim_before, trim_after, issues_resolved[], similarity_before/after, source_transcript_text_before/after}]`, `rejected_nudges[]`. Issue codes observed: `mid_sentence_end`, `mid_sentence_start`, `boilerplate_start`.
- Pipeline steps run (from `hype.metadata.json`): `transcribe → scenes → shots → arrange → cut`; tool_versions dict tracks every script hash. `refine` and `validate` also exist as stages (see logs dir). This tells me the pipeline has per-stage caching and a versioned metadata manifest I must preserve.
- Existing tests (from `tools/tests/`): `test_refine.py`, `test_arrangement_schema.py`, `test_schema_contract.py`, `test_hype_cut_invariants.py`, `test_pipeline_caching.py` — these are my validation surface; I will extend them rather than creating parallel suites.

**Constraints (per brief).**
- No arrangement schema changes beyond optional warning fields.
- No Remotion changes.
- No new LLM call sites (reviewers must use deterministic signals only).
- `inspect_cut.py` must be stdlib-only.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current modules and confirm assumptions (`tools/pipeline.py`, `tools/refine.py`, `tools/arrange.py`, `tools/cut.py`, `tools/timeline.py`, `tools/transcribe.py`, `tools/scenes.py`, `tools/text_match.py`, `tools/tests/test_refine.py`, `tools/tests/test_pipeline_caching.py`, `tools/tests/test_arrangement_schema.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Read** the stage registry in `tools/pipeline.py` — identify how stages are declared, how they receive/write their run dir, and how tool-version hashes are recorded in `metadata.json["pipeline"]["tool_versions"]` so `quality_zones.py` slots in next to `transcribe.py`/`scenes.py`/`shots.py` without breaking cache invalidation.
2. **Read** `tools/refine.py` to catalogue (a) every existing issue code (confirmed so far: `mid_sentence_end`, `mid_sentence_start`, `boilerplate_start`, `no_valid_nudge`), (b) how nudges mutate `arrangement.json` vs writing `refine.json`, (c) how transcript lookup is done (likely through `text_match.py` / the cached `transcript.json`), and (d) the iteration/convergence loop. This is the reviewer I'm keeping — I need a clean extraction point for its logic.
3. **Read** `tools/transcribe.py` and `tools/scenes.py` to see (a) how they scope caches per source (likely by source sha256 under `runs/<source_slug>/`), (b) whether they expose a callable API or only a CLI. `quality_zones.py` should mirror whichever pattern is canonical.
4. **Read** `tools/timeline.py` and the cut stage to understand how trim ranges land on the timeline — this is what `visual_quality` and `overlay_fit` reviewers need to cross-check against quality zones.
5. **Read** `tools/tests/test_refine.py` and `test_pipeline_caching.py` to see how they mock run dirs and how caching-by-source-hash is exercised; new tests follow these patterns.
6. **Capture** the concrete answers inline at the top of this plan before starting Step 2. If any assumption (see Assumptions below) is wrong, stop and raise before implementing.

### Step 2: Define the shared data model (`tools/enriched_arrangement.py` — new)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `tools/enriched_arrangement.py` exposing dataclasses (stdlib `dataclasses`, no deps): `EnrichedArrangement`, `EnrichedClip`, `QualityZone`, `ReviewerFinding`, `ReviewerResult`, and enums `ZoneKind` (`audio_dead` | `video_dead`) and `FindingSeverity` (`auto_fix` | `flag`).
2. **Implement** `load(run_dir: Path) -> EnrichedArrangement` that reads `arrangement.json`, `hype.timeline.json` (if present), each source's `quality_zones.json` (if present), and the per-clip source transcript/scene caches, producing one in-memory object reviewers operate on. No JSON side effects.
3. **Implement** `write_back(enriched, run_dir)` that persists only what is allowed to change: optional `warnings: list[str]` field per clip in `arrangement.json` (the one additive schema change the brief allows) plus `refine.json` and `quality_zones.json` files. It must NOT alter any existing arrangement fields it doesn't own.
4. **Keep** the module <300 lines; this is plumbing, not logic.

### Step 3: Implement the `quality_zones` ingest stage (`tools/quality_zones.py` — new, `tools/pipeline.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tools/quality_zones.py` with a `compute(source_path: Path, cache_dir: Path) -> QualityZonesReport` entrypoint and an `argparse`-based `__main__` matching the CLI shape of `transcribe.py`/`scenes.py`.
2. **Detect** `audio_dead` zones by piping the source through `ffmpeg -af silencedetect=n=-35dB:d=0.5 -f null -` (stdlib `subprocess`, parse stderr for `silence_start=`/`silence_end=`). This is the simplest zero-new-dep option and matches how `scenes.py` already shells out.
3. **Detect** `video_dead` zones via `ffmpeg -vf blackdetect=d=0.3:pic_th=0.98 -f null -` (parse `black_start`/`black_end`/`black_duration`). Both thresholds configurable via CLI flags with the defaults above.
4. **Cache** output at `runs/<source_slug>/quality_zones.json` keyed by source sha256; on cache hit, skip the ffmpeg call. Mirror the contract of `transcribe.py`/`scenes.py` exactly so `test_pipeline_caching.py` patterns extend cleanly.
5. **Wire** the stage into `pipeline.py` next to `shots` (after `scenes`, before `arrange`) and add `"quality_zones.py"` to `metadata.json["pipeline"]["tool_versions"]` emission and to `steps_run`.
6. **Expose** zones in `EnrichedArrangement.load()` by joining on source slug so each clip knows which zones overlap its trim range.

### Step 4: Refactor `refine.py` into multi-reviewer (`tools/refine.py`, `tools/reviewers/` — new subpackage)
**Scope:** Large

1. **Extract** the existing audio-boundary logic from `refine.py` into `tools/reviewers/audio_boundary.py`, preserving issue codes `mid_sentence_end`, `mid_sentence_start`, `boilerplate_start`, `no_valid_nudge` and the `trim_before`/`trim_after`/`similarity_*`/`source_transcript_text_*` output shape used in `refine.json` (brief run confirms this is load-bearing for downstream inspection).
2. **Define** a reviewer protocol in `tools/reviewers/__init__.py`:
   ```python
   class Reviewer(Protocol):
       name: str
       def review(self, enriched: EnrichedArrangement) -> list[ReviewerFinding]: ...
   ```
   Each `ReviewerFinding` carries `clip_order`, `code`, `severity` (`auto_fix` | `flag`), optional `proposed_patch` (only set when severity=`auto_fix`), plus human message. This is the split the brief asks for: `auto_fix` findings mutate arrangement; `flag` findings only surface.
3. **Add** `tools/reviewers/visual_quality.py`: checks each clip's visual trim range against its source's `video_dead` zones; emits `flag` (`visual_dead_overlap`) when overlap > 15% of clip duration. Deterministic; no LLM.
4. **Add** `tools/reviewers/speaker_flow.py`: checks the dialogue track for speaker-change discontinuities using scene/shot boundaries from the existing caches — e.g., same `pool_id` prefix but non-contiguous `trim_sub_range` without an intervening b-roll stinger. Emits `flag` (`speaker_jumpcut`) when detected. No audio analysis beyond what's already cached.
5. **Add** `tools/reviewers/overlay_fit.py`: checks that for each `overlay` role clip the visual source is NOT the same `pool_id` as the dialogue underneath, and that the visual duration on the timeline covers ≥90% of the underlying audio clip. Emits `auto_fix` (`overlay_too_short`) with a `proposed_patch` that extends the visual's trim when a source slack exists; `flag` otherwise.
6. **Rewrite** `refine.py` to: (a) instantiate the four reviewers, (b) run them against `EnrichedArrangement`, (c) apply `auto_fix` findings in priority order (`audio_boundary` → `overlay_fit`, iterating up to 3 passes like today), (d) write `refine.json` with **two top-level keys** — `auto_fixes` (applied, shaped like today's `per_clip`) and `flags` (unapplied, new list per reviewer). `iterations_run`, `converged`, `rejected_nudges` stay for back-compat with existing readers.
7. **Preserve** `refine.json`'s per-clip output shape for `audio_boundary` entries so `inspect_cut.py` and any downstream reader can still ingest old runs.

### Step 5: Implement `inspect_cut.py` (`tools/inspect_cut.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tools/inspect_cut.py` as a stdlib-only (`argparse`, `json`, `pathlib`, `textwrap`, `sys`, ANSI via plain strings — no `rich`, `click`, `curses`, `blessed`) CLI. Invocation: `python -m tools.inspect_cut <run_dir>` with optional `--clip <order>` for zoom-in and `--no-color` for plain output.
2. **Zoomed-out SCRIPT view**: a reading-order list of dialogue clips with each `source_transcript_text` (from arrangement + enriched cache), separated by visual-stinger placeholders. Warnings from reviewers render inline as `⚠ [code] message` after the affected clip.
3. **Zoomed-out STRUCTURE view**: a proportional ASCII timeline showing `a1`/`v1`/`v2` tracks with clip boundaries and quality-zone overlays (`░` for `audio_dead`, `▒` for `video_dead`). Source from `hype.timeline.json`.
4. **Zoom-in per-clip view** (`--clip N`): dumps all reviewer findings for that clip, the source transcript before/after, the trim range, any quality-zone overlap, and a **"fix options"** block listing `auto_fix` patches the reviewer proposed (purely informational — no mutation; mutation stays in `refine.py`).
5. **Keep** the file <400 lines; no new deps. Exit code 0 on success, 2 when any `flag` findings exist (so it composes in CI).

### Step 6: Extend tests and prove the change (`tools/tests/test_refine.py`, `tools/tests/test_arrangement_schema.py`, `tools/tests/test_pipeline_caching.py`, `tools/tests/test_quality_zones.py` — new, `tools/tests/test_inspect_cut.py` — new, `tools/tests/test_enriched_arrangement.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Run** `pytest tools/tests/test_refine.py -x` first — it's the biggest behavior change and fastest to pin.
2. **Extend** `test_refine.py` to cover: four reviewers run, `auto_fixes` vs `flags` separation, `audio_boundary` output shape preserved, idempotent second pass.
3. **Add** `test_quality_zones.py` that exercises the ffmpeg-parse logic against a captured stderr fixture (so tests stay hermetic — no ffmpeg call in CI) and verifies the cache read/write contract.
4. **Add** `test_inspect_cut.py` covering: zoomed-out render for a known run, `--clip` zoom-in, exit code 2 when a flag is present, `--no-color` stripping ANSI.
5. **Add** `test_enriched_arrangement.py` covering the `load` / `write_back` round-trip and the "only `warnings[]` added" schema invariant.
6. **Extend** `test_arrangement_schema.py` to assert `warnings` is optional and schema remains backward-compatible with the existing brief run's arrangement.
7. **Extend** `test_pipeline_caching.py` so `quality_zones` participates in the same cache-invalidation logic as `transcribe`/`scenes`.
8. **Final** sweep: `pytest tools/tests/ -x` and a manual run of `python -m tools.inspect_cut tools/runs/ados_pool/briefs/brief` using the existing artifacts to sanity-check the visualizer against real data.

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (audit) must land first and may invalidate assumptions — stop and reconcile before coding.
2. Step 2 (shared model) — everything downstream imports this.
3. Step 3 (quality_zones) — produces a new artifact `EnrichedArrangement` needs.
4. Step 4 (refine refactor) — depends on 2 and 3.
5. Step 5 (inspect_cut) — depends on 2, 3, 4 being stable (reads what they write).
6. Step 6 (tests) — extended alongside each step in practice but run as a single final gate.

## Validation Order
1. Targeted: `pytest tools/tests/test_enriched_arrangement.py tools/tests/test_quality_zones.py` — cheap, isolate foundation.
2. `pytest tools/tests/test_refine.py` — biggest risk.
3. `pytest tools/tests/test_inspect_cut.py`.
4. Full suite: `pytest tools/tests/ -x`.
5. Manual: `python -m tools.pipeline runs/ados_pool/briefs/brief --resume` completes without regressing the existing brief run, and `python -m tools.inspect_cut runs/ados_pool/briefs/brief` renders script + structure + at least one `--clip` zoom.

    {
      "id": "DEBT-030",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the storage/versioning sequence still has a race. step 3 uploads files under `post-bundles/bundles/<post_id>/<next_version>/...` before calling `register_bundle_version(...)`, but step 1 also makes the rpc compute `version = max(version) + 1` itself. concurrent uploads can therefore diverge between storage prefix and db version, and any rpc failure after upload leaves orphaned files because storage writes happened before the transactional sql step.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: compared step 3.11 in `plan_v6.md` with the vendored `tools/_reference/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/timelinerenderer.tsx:66-85`. the plan's scaled visual branch wraps a bare `<visualclip .../>` in `<absolutefill style={{transform: scale(...)}}>` but does not include a surrounding `<sequence from=... durationinframes=...>`. in the reference, that branch wraps `<visualclipsequence .../>`, which is where clip timing is applied, so implementing the plan literally would make scaled non-position-override clips mount from frame 0 for the full composition instead of honoring `clip.at` and `getclipdurationinframes(...)`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-of-a-4-sprint-plan-20260421-0520"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-058",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the proposed cache contract for the `cut` step is technically unsafe because it uses only `hype.timeline.json` as the sentinel even though `cut.py` writes `hype.timeline.json`, `hype.assets.json`, and `hype.metadata.json` as one unit (`tools/cut.py:763-769`). later planned steps consume the extra outputs as required inputs (`tools/render_remotion.py:62-99` requires `--assets`, and the bridge copies `hype.assets.json`), so a partial prior run or manual deletion of assets/metadata would still be treated as 'cached' and cause downstream breakage.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-final-of-a-4-sprint-20260421-0928"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-068",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: flagged that `--print-sql` still defaults `--timeline-id` to `<name>` even though `public.timelines.id` is a uuid column, so the default emitted `update` is not runnable as written.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-final-of-a-4-sprint-20260421-0928"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-069",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: flagged that byte-preserving `hype.assets.json` into `public.timelines` remains technically incomplete because reigh-app resolves asset `file` values as http urls or `timeline-assets` bucket keys, while `cut.py` writes local absolute paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-final-of-a-4-sprint-20260421-0928"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the actual migration at `/users/user_c042661f/documents/banodoco-workspace/supabase/migrations/20260325070000_create_posts.sql`. the repository already defines `posts`, `post_media`, and `post_assets`, with `posts.body`, `posts.slug`, `posts.status` allowing `'archived'`, and ownership/rls keyed through `members.auth_user_id`; the plan still treats that schema as unreadable and proposes a materially different surface (`body_md`, `author_user_id`, draft/published-only status), which is not the minimal extension the brief asked for.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the brief says to reuse the existing join tables, but the live schema is `post_media(post_id, media_id, sort_order, caption)` and `post_assets(post_id, asset_id)`. the plan instead assumes generic `position` columns and ownership checks built around `profiles.discord_id`, so it is not aligned with the repository's current posts infrastructure.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-016",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still only writes `post_media` / `post_assets` rows during the explicit publish action via `syncembeds`, even though the brief also requires editing existing posts and step 8 says edit-mode saves preserve the current status. that leaves the repository's existing join-table requirement only partially covered for ordinary edits, because a published post can be saved with a new body before its join rows are updated.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-027",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still treats markdown-mode ownership of `/submit/post` and `/posts/:slug` as parallel-branch work, but those surfaces already exist in this repo: `src/app.tsx:42-53` already wires the routes and `src/pages/submitpost/index.tsx:59-380` already owns the markdown draft/publish flow. bundle mode therefore needs an explicit integration story for the existing editor now, not merge-later language.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-028",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the `admin_status` fix is still only partial. step 4 hides bundle bytes for `admin_status='hidden'`, but the surrounding post page is still expected to load through `usepost`, and `src/hooks/usepost.ts:223-229` plus the live posts rls in `supabase/migrations/20260325070000_create_posts.sql:21-24` still allow any published post row to be read. a hidden bundle post could therefore still leak title/body/post chrome even if the iframe itself 404s.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-048",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: deliverable 3 says the python wrapper may pass props inline or via a props file, but step 4.2 writes a `.remotion-props.json` file and step 4.3 still invokes `npx remotion render ... --props props_path`. that is an internal contract mismatch in the approved plan itself: the command is treating a filename like an inline props argument, so the wrapper path is still only partially specified against the requested cli behavior.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "sprint-2-of-a-4-sprint-plan-20260421-0520"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the actual reigh-app integration path and found that the editor is not file-backed. `usetimelineslist()` lists and creates rows in `public.timelines` with `project_id`, `user_id`, default `config`, and default `asset_registry` (`reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelineslist.ts:11-55`), while `supabasedataprovider` only loads and saves `config` and `asset_registry` from that table (`reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/data/supabasedataprovider.ts:51-109,192-204`; schema in `reigh-app/supabase/migrations/20260325090000_create_video_editor_tables.sql:1-27`). the plan's `open_in_reigh.py` copy-to-`public/timelines` or `public/demos` bridge does not match that storage model, so against the current repository it would produce inert files rather than something the editor can open.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the main caller mismatch now is between step 8's save flow and step 11's renderer. the editor updates `posts.body` on autosave/save but only runs `syncembeds` on publish, while `postdetail` is supposed to resolve `::art` / `::resource` / `::media` tokens through `post_media` and `post_assets`; for an already published post, that lets the live body and the join tables diverge after an edit.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: there is also still a caller-level count mismatch on the owner's profile. step 12 says the owner-facing `/:username/posts` tab should call `useposts({ memberid: profilememberid, includedrafts: isownprofile })`, which includes drafts for the owner, but the same step keeps `useuserprofile.postcount` on the public-only `status='published' and admin_status != 'hidden'` predicate. on an own profile with drafts, the badge and the visible card count would still disagree.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked `/2rp` callers around `handlecreateclick` and `authactionmodal`. today every caller only passes `'art'` or `'resource'`, and the modal label branch only handles those two values, so widening the union for posts also requires updating the supporting caller/ui branch or the new post cta will fall through to the wrong copy.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the existing caller surface is still the markdown submit page. `src/pages/submitpost/index.tsx:212-350` already calls `createdraft`, `savedraft`, `syncembeds`, and `publishpost`, and the plan only says bundle upload will live as a sub-component or stopgap route without spelling out how those existing callers branch once a post is `render_mode='bundle'`. as written, the same post can still be reopened in the markdown editor and continue writing `body` / embed links unless the caller ui is explicitly changed.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-035",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the preview url return contract is underspecified for current callers. `publishpost` is where a slug is persisted today (`src/lib/posts.ts:90-108`), but bundle uploads can happen against drafts that still have `slug = null`; the plan says `process-bundle` returns `/posts/<slug>?preview=<bv>` without naming the helper or query that produces a stable slug for unpublished posts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-045",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: onshipped callback semantics are now spelled out in step 13.3: `handlevibeshipped(_result: { bundleversionid: string })`. plan chose not to navigate to /posts/<slug> (explicit rationale: post is in pending review, has no slug until approve). that side-steps the slug-ambiguity concern from onshipped-slug-1. sound choice, but see the correctness finding above: the `refreshpost()` in the body is not a real helper, so the chosen flow (stay on submitpost, let isexistingbundlepost flip via refetch) doesn't actually work without additional wiring. addresses the slug side but opens a refresh-mechanism side.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-vibe-mode-for-bundle-20260421-0421"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-052",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual scaled-track caller in `tools/_reference/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/timelinerenderer.tsx:54-85`. the wrapper does not call a bare `visualclip`; it calls `visualclipsequence`, and that sequence object is what carries `clip.at` and `getclipdurationinframes(clip, fps)` into remotion. step 3.11 in the plan changes that caller contract to `<absolutefill ...><visualclip .../></absolutefill>`, so it no longer handles the timing arguments the real caller relies on.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-of-a-4-sprint-plan-20260421-0520"
      ]
    },
    {

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: flagged that the live creation caller still expects more than the bridge provides: `usetimelineslist().createtimeline()` inserts `project_id`, `user_id`, `name`, `config`, and `asset_registry`, while the planned helper only emits an `update` for an existing row id.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

    },
    {

      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]

  "post-ship-it-refresh-path-is-missing": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-046",
      "concern": "post-ship-it refresh path is missing: step 13.3's `handlevibeshipped` body calls `refreshpost()` to flip `isexistingbundlepost` via a usepost refetch, but src/hooks/usepost.ts (lines 7-15 `usepostresult` interface, line 372 return) does not export any refetch/refresh helper \u2014 the fetch is a one-shot `useeffect` keyed on `[slugorid]` (l206). without a refetch, `existingpost.render_mode` stays stale after ship it, `isexistingbundlepost` never becomes true, and the auto-switch effect at src/pages/submitpost/index.tsx:559-563 never fires. the ui would remain in vibe mode on a post whose server state is now a shipped bundle. fix requires one of: (a) add a `refetch: () => void` export to usepost (small shipped-file change, probably the cleanest and useful outside vibe too), (b) in handlevibeshipped, call `setmode('bundle')` directly and accept stale `isexistingbundlepost` until the next full mount, or (c) navigate to the same url with a cache-bust param to force usepost remount. plan must commit to one and name the exact code change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-vibe-mode-for-bundle-20260421-0421"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "DEBT-037",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: there is still a schema-level dependency gap against the actual repository. the committed posts migration in `supabase/migrations/20260325070000_create_posts.sql:4-12` only creates `id`, `member_id`, `title`, `body`, `slug`, `status`, `created_at`, and `updated_at`, while the plan assumes `posts.admin_status`, `posts.cover_media_id`, and `posts.published_at` already exist and only adds `render_mode` plus `active_bundle_version_id`. unless another migration lands first, this bundle plan is not self-contained against the checked-in schema.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-044",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: post-ship state refresh: the missing refetch on `usepost` is a broader symptom. the repo's existing `bundleupload.tsx` likely has a similar re-render gap after its own successful upload (verified by prior critiques that noted `loadlatestbundle` inside bundleupload rather than relying on usepost refetch). vibe's ship it reuses the same server path but lacks a drop-in equivalent of bundleupload's internal re-fetch logic. plan should explicitly cite how bundleupload refreshes its own view after a successful upload and apply the same pattern, or extend usepost with a refetch (benefits both surfaces). either way, this isn't a vibe-only gap \u2014 it's a pattern the repo has had to solve before.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: searched the sibling audio renderer and the same playback-rate contract is broader than the visual path that was just corrected. the vendored `tools/_reference/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/audiotrack.tsx:29-53` computes `playbackrate = getsanitizedplaybackrate(clip.speed)` and passes it into the audio component, but step 3.10 in `plan_v6.md` reduces `src/audiotrack.tsx` to `<audio src={fileurl} {...computemediatrim(clip, fps)} volume={effectivevolume} />` with no `playbackrate`. that means the plan only partially ports the per-clip speed behavior required for sprint 2.",

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the reigh integration problem is broader than probe order. the editor loads timeline data through `loadtimelinejsonfromprovider(provider, timelineid)`, which calls `provider.loadtimeline(timelineid)` and `provider.loadassetregistry(timelineid)` (`reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts:388-402`), so the real integration boundary is a provider-backed timeline row, not a filesystem drop target. treating the problem as 'find the right folder under `public/`' narrows the issue incorrectly and misses the live persistence layer.",

        Debt guidance:
        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.

The proposed `overlay_fit` reviewer cannot safely emit an `auto_fix` that extends a visual trim under the current schema, because `Arrangement.visual_source` only stores `{pool_id, role}` and `build_multitrack_timeline()` always derives the overlay clip from the pool entry's full source range; there is no writable visual trim field to patch without violating the plan's own \"no arrangement schema changes beyond optional warning fields\" constraint.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked the current pool data model in `tools/pool_build.py:103-128` and the live `tools/runs/ados_pool/pool.json`. The proposed `speaker_flow` heuristic is not technically grounded as written: dialogue pool ids are unique synthetic ids like `pool_d_0008`, not reusable speaker identifiers, and the actual speaker signal lives separately on optional `speaker` fields (with some `None` values), so using a shared pool-id prefix plus non-contiguous trims would not reliably detect speaker continuity problems.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked how `hype.metadata.json` is written in `tools/cut.py:503-590,887-980` and rewritten by `tools/refine.py:243-250,398-425`. The plan says to record `quality_zones` in metadata via `pipeline.py`, but `pipeline.py` only launches subprocesses; the actual `steps_run` block and `tool_versions` map are emitted inside `cut.py`/`refine.py`, and `_tool_fingerprints()` is a hard-coded allowlist that currently omits both `refine.py` and any future `quality_zones.py`. As written, the plan under-specifies the metadata changes needed for the new stage to show up correctly.","flagged":true}]},{"id":"scope","question":"Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?","findings":[{"detail":"Checked the existing visual/timing policy in `tools/cut.py:614-618,752-766` and its tests in `tools/tests/test_multitrack_cut.py`. The repository already enforces overlay coverage and freeze rules during `compile_arrangement_plan()` via `_MIN_OVERLAY_COVERAGE_SEC = 2.5` and `_MAX_VISUAL_HOLD_RATIO = 0.2`; the plan adds a second `overlay_fit`/`visual_quality` policy layer with different thresholds like 90 percent coverage and dead-zone overlap, but it does not say which layer is authoritative or how conflicting thresholds should be reconciled.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked media provenance across `tools/pool_build.py`, `tools/cut.py`, and the live `tools/runs/ados_pool/pool.json`. The underlying concept in this repo is asset-level provenance (`entry['asset']`, registry asset keys, per-asset source metadata), not source-slug provenance; the current pool build path even emits only `asset: 'main'`. That means the plan's repeated \"join on source slug\" framing is narrower than the actual concept the code handles, and multi-asset support would need broader asset-key propagation than the plan calls out.","flagged":true}]},{"id":"all_locations","question":"Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?","findings":[{"detail":"Checked `tools/pipeline.py:16-52,351-646,748-772`. Adding a new source-stage is not just a `build_pool_steps()` edit: the step name is also constrained by `STEP_ORDER`, `PER_SOURCE_SENTINELS`, `--from`/`--skip` validation, resume logic, and the per-brief/per-source log/sentinel routing. The plan mentions `pipeline.py` generally, but it does not enumerate these supporting constants and control paths even though they are the real registration surface for a new pipeline stage.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked metadata ref plumbing in `tools/cut.py:513-544,937-980` and `tools/refine.py:243-250`. Source-stage artifact refs are currently threaded only for `scenes_ref`, `transcript_ref`, and `shots_ref`; there is no generic ref mechanism, no `quality_zones_ref`, and refine's `_regen_args()` also hard-codes the same set. If `inspect_cut.py` or the shared enriched model is supposed to discover per-source zone files through existing metadata, that supporting infrastructure is currently missing from the plan.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked arrangement save/load call sites via `rg` across `tools/arrange.py`, `tools/cut.py`, `tools/refine.py`, and tests. Every arrangement write goes through `timeline.save_arrangement()` and every read goes through `timeline.load_arrangement()`, so even a minimal additive `warnings` field requires coordinated updates in `timeline.py` before any new module can persist it; the plan names a new `write_back()` helper, but the existing shared validator remains the real choke point.","flagged":true}]},{"id":"callers","question":"Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?","findings":[{"detail":"Checked callers of `build_metadata_from_arrangement()` in `tools/cut.py:1338-1344`, `tools/refine.py:401-414`, and `tools/tests/test_refine.py:160-177` / `test_multitrack_cut.py:173-190`. Those callers pass argparse-like objects with `scenes`, `transcript`, `shots`, `arrangement`, `renderer`, and `out`, but nothing for quality zones, so a metadata-level `quality_zones_ref` cannot be added in one place only; the caller contracts themselves would need to grow.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked callers of `compile_arrangement_plan()` and `build_multitrack_timeline()` in `tools/arrange.py`, `tools/cut.py`, `tools/refine.py`, and `tools/tests/test_multitrack_cut.py`. Every caller passes arrangement clips whose `visual_source` is only `{pool_id, role}` and whose `audio_source` is the only place with a trim range, so the proposed `overlay_fit` fix path is not something the existing caller contracts can express or round-trip.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked how the source-analysis stages are invoked in `tools/pipeline.py:299-351,434-578`. The current callers always execute `transcribe.py`, `scenes.py`, and `shots.py` as subprocess CLIs; neither stage is wired into the pipeline through a shared Python `compute(...)` API. That means the proposed `quality_zones.compute(source_path, cache_dir)` helper could be useful internally, but it does not actually match an existing caller contract and should not be mistaken for the integration point that pipeline resume/caching depends on.","flagged":true}]}],"flags":[{"id":"FLAG-001","concern":"Arrangement visual fixes: overlay_fit auto-fix has no writable place to persist a visual trim in the current arrangement schema.","category":"correctness","severity_hint":"likely-significant","evidence":"`tools/timeline.py:321-327` limits `ArrangementVisualSource` to `{pool_id, role}`, and `tools/cut.py:793-835` always derives `clip_v2_*` from the pool entry rather than an arrangement-level visual trim."},{"id":"FLAG-002","concern":"Metadata wiring: quality_zones will not appear in `hype.metadata.json` unless `cut.py`/`refine.py` metadata builders and `_tool_fingerprints()` are updated; You are an independent reviewer. Critique the plan against the actual repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/tools





        Idea:
Build three unified deliverables: (1) quality_zones.py ingest stage writing audio_dead + video_dead zones per source, wired into pipeline; (2) refactor refine.py into multi-reviewer with audio_boundary (existing) + visual_quality + speaker_flow + overlay_fit, splitting output into auto_fixes vs flags; (3) new inspect_cut.py CLI rendering SCRIPT + STRUCTURE zoomed-out and per-clip zoom-in with warnings and fix options. All three share a common enriched-arrangement data model. Scope: no arrangement schema changes beyond adding optional warning fields, no Remotion changes, no new LLM call sites, stdlib-only visualizer.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: quality_zones + multi-reviewer refine + inspect_cut (v2)

## Overview

**Root-cause check.** None of the 17 flags point at the wrong code or wrong root cause — they point at concrete mechanics in the existing repo the v1 plan glossed over. Two specific re-scopings are called for:

1. **`overlay_fit` must be flag-only.** `Arrangement.visual_source` is `{pool_id, role}` (`tools/timeline.py:321-327`) and `build_multitrack_timeline()` derives the overlay clip from the pool entry (`tools/cut.py:793-835`). There is no writable visual-trim field, and the brief forbids schema growth beyond optional warnings. So `overlay_fit` emits `flag` findings only.
2. **`speaker_flow` must key on `pool.speaker`, not pool-id prefix.** `tools/pool_build.py:103-128` makes each `pool_d_####` id unique per quote; the real speaker identity is the optional `speaker` field on each pool entry (`None` on some). The reviewer uses `pool.speaker` when present and emits nothing when absent.

Everything else is depth: the plan must enumerate the real surfaces — `timeline.py` save/load, `cut.py` / `refine.py` metadata builders and `_tool_fingerprints()`, `pipeline.py` `STEP_ORDER` / `PER_SOURCE_SENTINELS` / resume — and baseline the test gate instead of claiming a green suite.

**Idea (unchanged):**
1. `tools/quality_zones.py` — ingest stage emitting `audio_dead` + `video_dead` zones per source, wired into the pipeline.
2. Refactor `tools/refine.py` into a multi-reviewer (`audio_boundary` existing, `visual_quality`, `speaker_flow`, `overlay_fit` new) with `auto_fixes` vs `flags` split.
3. `tools/inspect_cut.py` — stdlib-only visualizer (SCRIPT + STRUCTURE zoomed-out, per-clip zoom-in).
4. All three consume one shared `EnrichedArrangement` data model.

**Non-negotiable constraints (from brief):** no arrangement schema changes beyond optional warning fields; no Remotion changes; no new LLM call sites; stdlib-only visualizer.

**Repo facts confirmed from the brief run's artifacts and gate critique:**
- Arrangement schema: `tools/timeline.py:321-327,553-648` (`ArrangementVisualSource` = `{pool_id, role}`, `save_arrangement()` / `load_arrangement()` is the choke point).
- Metadata emission: `tools/cut.py:503-590,887-980` writes `hype.metadata.json`; `tools/refine.py:243-250,398-425` regenerates it via `build_metadata_from_arrangement()`; `_tool_fingerprints()` is a hard-coded allowlist.
- Pipeline stage registration: `tools/pipeline.py:16-52,299-351,434-578,748-772` (`STEP_ORDER`, `PER_SOURCE_SENTINELS`, `--from`/`--skip` validation, resume, per-source/per-brief log and sentinel routing).
- Existing policy: `tools/cut.py:614-618,752-766` already enforces `_MIN_OVERLAY_COVERAGE_SEC = 2.5` and `_MAX_VISUAL_HOLD_RATIO = 0.2`. The new `overlay_fit` reviewer *reuses these same constants* rather than inventing new thresholds.
- Provenance: `entry['asset']` (asset-key) is the join field, not source-slug (`tools/pool_build.py`, `tools/cut.py`). Plan uses asset-key wording throughout.
- Test baseline (reported by gate): `pytest tools/tests -q` currently shows ~29 pre-existing failures. Our test gate is therefore **no new regressions vs. baseline + specific suites green**, not "entire suite passes".

## Phase 1: Foundation — audit, schema field, shared model

### Step 1: Audit + capture test baseline (`tools/pipeline.py`, `tools/refine.py`, `tools/cut.py`, `tools/timeline.py`, `tools/arrange.py`, `tools/pool_build.py`, `tools/transcribe.py`, `tools/scenes.py`, `tools/shots.py`, `tools/tests/`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Read** the exact regions flagged by the gate and write a short `.megaplan/audit.md` in the run dir (not committed) pinning down:
   - `tools/timeline.py:321-327` (`ArrangementVisualSource`, `ArrangementAudioSource`, `ArrangementClip` dataclasses).
   - `tools/timeline.py:553-648` (`save_arrangement()` / `load_arrangement()` — the arrangement schema validator and roundtrip).
   - `tools/cut.py:503-590,614-618,677-852,887-980,1338-1344` (metadata builder, overlay policy, `build_multitrack_timeline()`, `build_metadata_from_arrangement()` call site).
   - `tools/refine.py:243-250,398-425,401-414` (`_regen_args()` ref plumbing, metadata regeneration).
   - `tools/pipeline.py:16-52,299-351,434-578,748-772` (`STEP_ORDER`, `PER_SOURCE_SENTINELS`, `build_pool_steps()`, `--from`/`--skip`, resume, log routing).
   - `tools/pool_build.py:103-128` (pool-entry `speaker` field).
   - `_tool_fingerprints()` definition (grep — probably in `cut.py`).
2. **Capture** the test baseline: run `pytest tools/tests -q > .megaplan/baseline.txt` from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/` and extract the set of **currently-failing test nodeids**. This is the reference set Step 11 diffs against. Do not try to "fix" pre-existing failures — only guard against adding new ones.
3. **Confirm** or reject the four v1 assumptions the gate didn't move on (ffmpeg dependency, asset-key provenance, stdlib-only visualizer, no LLM). If any is wrong, stop and raise.

### Step 2: Add optional `warnings` field to the arrangement schema (`tools/timeline.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** the `ArrangementClip` dataclass at `tools/timeline.py:321-327` with `warnings: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)`. This is the *only* additive change the brief permits on `arrangement.json`.
2. **Update** `save_arrangement()` / `load_arrangement()` at `tools/timeline.py:553-648` to (a) accept and emit `warnings` when non-empty, (b) treat missing `warnings` as `[]` on load — preserving back-compat with the existing `runs/ados_pool/briefs/brief/arrangement.json` which has no such field.
3. **Add** validation that `warnings` is a flat list of strings (no structured objects — richer finding data goes in `refine.json`).
4. **Run** `pytest tools/tests/test_arrangement_schema.py tools/tests/test_arrange.py tools/tests/test_multitrack_cut.py tools/tests/test_hype_cut_invariants.py -x` and update any test that round-trips an arrangement but did not tolerate the new key. These are the arrangement-roundtrip surfaces the gate called out in `issue_hints-1` / `all_locations-3`.

### Step 3: Shared data model (`tools/enriched_arrangement.py` — new)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `tools/enriched_arrangement.py` (stdlib-only; `dataclasses`, `pathlib`, `json`, `typing`). Exports:
   - Dataclasses: `EnrichedArrangement`, `EnrichedClip`, `QualityZone`, `ReviewerFinding`, `ReviewerResult`.
   - Enums: `ZoneKind` (`audio_dead` | `video_dead`), `FindingSeverity` (`auto_fix` | `flag`).
2. **Implement** `load(run_dir: Path) -> EnrichedArrangement` that reads (via existing helpers when possible) `arrangement.json`, `hype.timeline.json` (if present), per-asset `quality_zones.json` (if present), and the transcript/scenes caches per-asset. Joins use **asset-key** (`entry['asset']`, defaulting to `"main"`) — not source-slug — per gate flag `scope-2`.
3. **Implement** `apply_auto_fixes(enriched, findings)` and `collect_flags(enriched, findings)` as pure in-memory helpers. Persistence flows through `timeline.save_arrangement()` (Step 2's updated writer) — **no parallel `write_back()`**. This resolves `all_locations-3`: there is exactly one writer for the arrangement schema.
4. **Keep** under ~300 lines.

## Phase 2: Ingest stage + pipeline/metadata wiring

### Step 4: `quality_zones.py` CLI (`tools/quality_zones.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tools/quality_zones.py` as a subprocess-style CLI mirroring `tools/transcribe.py`/`tools/scenes.py`/`tools/shots.py` (argparse, reads a source path, writes a cache file). This matches `callers-3` (existing stages are invoked as subprocess CLIs, not Python APIs).
2. **Detect** `audio_dead` zones via `ffmpeg -i <src> -af silencedetect=n=-35dB:d=0.5 -f null -` (parse stderr `silence_start=`/`silence_end=`), and `video_dead` zones via `ffmpeg -vf blackdetect=d=0.3:pic_th=0.98 -f null -` (parse `black_start`/`black_end`). Both thresholds exposed as `--silence-db`, `--silence-dur`, `--black-dur`, `--black-pic-th` flags with those defaults.
3. **Write** output to `runs/<source_slug>/quality_zones.json` with shape:
   ```json
   {"source_sha256": "…", "asset_key": "main", "zones": [{"kind": "audio_dead", "start": 12.3, "end": 13.0}, …]}
   ```
   Cache-key is `source_sha256`; on hit, skip ffmpeg. Mirrors `transcribe.py`/`scenes.py` caching convention.
4. **Expose** an internal `compute(source_path: Path) -> QualityZonesReport` helper used only by tests (subprocess CLI remains the pipeline integration point).

### Step 5: Pipeline stage registration (`tools/pipeline.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Register** `"quality_zones"` in `STEP_ORDER` at `tools/pipeline.py:16-52` between `"scenes"` and `"shots"` (sibling per-source analyzers).
2. **Add** `"quality_zones"` to `PER_SOURCE_SENTINELS` with sentinel name `quality_zones.done` (matching the `scenes.done`/`shots.done` pattern).
3. **Extend** `build_pool_steps()` at `tools/pipeline.py:299-351` to enqueue `quality_zones.py` for each source like `scenes.py`/`shots.py` — same per-source log dir, same sentinel gating, same `--from`/`--skip` validation at `tools/pipeline.py:434-578`, same resume logic and per-brief/per-source log routing at `:748-772`. This is the full registration surface `all_locations-1` enumerates.
4. **Confirm** that `pipeline.py` does not need to touch metadata — that is owned by `cut.py` / `refine.py` (Step 6).

### Step 6: Metadata wiring (`tools/cut.py`, `tools/refine.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Update** `_tool_fingerprints()` (the hard-coded allowlist — grep for it in `tools/cut.py`) to include `"quality_zones.py"` and `"refine.py"` (the gate noted `refine.py` is currently missing too). Also include `"inspect_cut.py"` and `"enriched_arrangement.py"` since those are new.
2. **Extend** `build_metadata_from_arrangement()` at `tools/cut.py:887-980` to accept a `quality_zones_refs: dict[str, Path]` argument (asset-key → file path) and emit it under `pipeline.sources[<asset>].quality_zones_ref` alongside the existing `scenes_ref`/`transcript_ref`/`shots_ref`. This is the generic ref mechanism `all_locations-2` asks for: one new named ref, keyed per asset.
3. **Update** the two `build_metadata_from_arrangement()` call sites — `tools/cut.py:1338-1344` and `tools/refine.py:401-414` — plus `refine.py`'s `_regen_args()` at `:243-250`. Both caller shims discover the per-source `quality_zones.json` from the run dir and pass the resulting dict. Callers-1 resolved: ref plumbing updated everywhere it is threaded.
4. **Add** `"quality_zones"` to `pipeline.steps_run` emission at `tools/cut.py:503-590`.
5. **Update** `tools/tests/test_refine.py:160-177` and `tools/tests/test_multitrack_cut.py:173-190` fixtures to provide the new argument (empty dict is acceptable when no zones file exists).

## Phase 3: Reviewers + refine orchestration

### Step 7: Reviewer protocol + audio_boundary extraction (`tools/reviewers/__init__.py`, `tools/reviewers/audio_boundary.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tools/reviewers/__init__.py` with the `Reviewer` Protocol:
   ```python
   class Reviewer(Protocol):
       name: str
       def review(self, enriched: EnrichedArrangement) -> list[ReviewerFinding]: ...
   ```
   `ReviewerFinding` fields: `clip_order`, `code`, `severity` (`auto_fix` | `flag`), optional `proposed_patch` (only populated when `severity=auto_fix`), `message`.
2. **Extract** the existing `refine.py` logic into `tools/reviewers/audio_boundary.py`, preserving issue codes `mid_sentence_end`, `mid_sentence_start`, `boilerplate_start`, `no_valid_nudge` and the per-clip output shape (`trim_before`/`trim_after`/`similarity_*`/`source_transcript_text_*`) observed in the live `runs/ados_pool/briefs/brief/refine.json`.
3. **Keep** the extracted module <250 lines.

### Step 8: Three new reviewers (`tools/reviewers/visual_quality.py`, `tools/reviewers/speaker_flow.py`, `tools/reviewers/overlay_fit.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **`visual_quality.py`**: for each clip, compute the visual trim range and find intersections with the asset's `video_dead` zones (from Step 4's cache). Emit `flag` `visual_dead_overlap` when overlap ≥ `max(0.5s, 15% of clip duration)`. `auto_fix` path: none (no writable visual-trim field; consistent with FLAG-001/correctness-1). Scope limited to dead-zone overlap per the idea brief's minimal framing (answers `scope-1` — no new policy layer).
2. **`speaker_flow.py`**: iterate arrangement dialogue clips in order; for each consecutive pair on the audio track look up each clip's `pool_entry.speaker` via the pool (passed through `EnrichedArrangement`). Emit `flag` `speaker_repeat_without_break` when two adjacent dialogue clips have the *same non-None speaker* with no intervening b-roll stinger AND non-contiguous `trim_sub_range`. Emit nothing when either speaker is `None` (this is the `correctness-2` fix: ground on `pool.speaker`, not pool-id prefix).
3. **`overlay_fit.py`**: **flag-only, no auto_fix.** For each `overlay`-role clip, emit `flag`s:
   - `overlay_same_source_as_audio` when `visual_source.pool_id == audio_source.pool_id`.
   - `overlay_insufficient_coverage` when the overlay clip duration `< _MIN_OVERLAY_COVERAGE_SEC` (imported from `tools/cut.py`) OR when hold ratio would exceed `_MAX_VISUAL_HOLD_RATIO` (also imported). Reusing the existing constants answers `scope-1` and `FLAG-001`/`correctness-1`/`callers-2`: no second, conflicting policy layer and no schema growth.
4. **Keep** each module <250 lines.

### Step 9: Refactor `refine.py` orchestration (`tools/refine.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Rewrite** the top level of `tools/refine.py` to:
   - Build `EnrichedArrangement` via `enriched_arrangement.load()`.
   - Instantiate the four reviewers and run them per iteration.
   - Apply `auto_fix` findings (today only `audio_boundary` produces these) — iterating up to 3 passes like today.
   - Persist arrangement changes through `timeline.save_arrangement()` (Step 2's updated writer) — not a parallel `write_back()`.
   - Write `refine.json` with top-level keys:
     ```json
     {
       "iterations_run": 3,
       "converged": false,
       "auto_fixes": {"audio_boundary": [...legacy per_clip shape...]},
       "flags": {"visual_quality": [...], "speaker_flow": [...], "overlay_fit": [...]},
       "rejected_nudges": [...],
       "per_clip": [...legacy mirror for back-compat...]
     }
     ```
     `per_clip` is preserved as a mirror of `auto_fixes["audio_boundary"]` so existing consumers (including `inspect_cut.py` reading older runs) stay green.
2. **Keep** `_regen_args()` and metadata regeneration (updated in Step 6) working in the new flow.
3. **Update** `tools/tests/test_refine.py` to cover: all four reviewers run, split correctness, legacy `per_clip` still present, audio_boundary idempotence on a second pass.

## Phase 4: Visualizer + test gate

### Step 10: `inspect_cut.py` (`tools/inspect_cut.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tools/inspect_cut.py`: stdlib-only (`argparse`, `json`, `pathlib`, `textwrap`, `sys`; ANSI via plain escape strings — no `rich`, `click`, `curses`). Entry: `python -m tools.inspect_cut <run_dir> [--clip N] [--no-color] [--json]`.
2. **SCRIPT view** (default): reading-order dialogue transcript sourced from `EnrichedArrangement`, with visual-stinger placeholders between dialogue clips. Inline warnings rendered as `⚠ [code] message` under each affected clip.
3. **STRUCTURE view**: proportional ASCII timeline across `a1`/`v1`/`v2` tracks, with quality-zone shading (`░` for `audio_dead`, `▒` for `video_dead`) overlaid on the relevant track. Source from `hype.timeline.json`.
4. **Zoom-in** (`--clip N`): dumps all reviewer findings for that clip, before/after transcript, trim range, zone overlaps, and an informational "fix options" block mirroring each `auto_fix` finding's `proposed_patch`. *No mutation* — mutation stays in `refine.py`.
5. **Exit codes**: `0` when no flags; `2` when any `flag` finding is present (composable in CI). `--json` emits the same data as structured JSON on stdout.
6. **Keep** under ~400 lines.

### Step 11: Test gate (`tools/tests/test_quality_zones.py`, `tools/tests/test_enriched_arrangement.py`, `tools/tests/test_inspect_cut.py` — new; extend `test_refine.py`, `test_arrangement_schema.py`, `test_multitrack_cut.py`, `test_hype_cut_invariants.py`, `test_pipeline_caching.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Run** cheap-first in this order (all from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/`):
   - `pytest tools/tests/test_enriched_arrangement.py tools/tests/test_quality_zones.py -x`
   - `pytest tools/tests/test_arrangement_schema.py tools/tests/test_arrange.py tools/tests/test_multitrack_cut.py tools/tests/test_hype_cut_invariants.py -x` (the arrangement-roundtrip surface from Step 2)
   - `pytest tools/tests/test_refine.py -x`
   - `pytest tools/tests/test_inspect_cut.py -x`
   - `pytest tools/tests/test_pipeline_caching.py -x`
2. **Whole suite diff**: run `pytest tools/tests -q` and compare failing nodeids to the Step 1 baseline. **Gate: the post-change failing set must be a (non-strict) subset of the baseline failing set** — i.e. zero new regressions. Pre-existing failures are not this plan's responsibility per FLAG-004/issue_hints-2.
3. **Schema round-trip test** (`test_arrangement_schema.py` extension): load `runs/ados_pool/briefs/brief/arrangement.json` (which lacks `warnings`) → should deserialize → re-serialize → diff-equal.
4. **Manual smoke**: `python -m tools.inspect_cut /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/tools/runs/ados_pool/briefs/brief` renders both views without error and surfaces at least one reviewer finding (the existing `validation.json` shows 8 `fail` clips, so we expect `visual_dead_overlap` and/or `overlay_same_source_as_audio` to fire on this known data).

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (audit + baseline) — pre-req for everything.
2. Step 2 (timeline schema field) — unlocks persistence for reviewer output.
3. Step 3 (shared data model) — consumed by all downstream files.
4. Step 4 (quality_zones CLI).
5. Step 5 (pipeline registration).
6. Step 6 (metadata wiring) — depends on 4 emitting the cache file.
7. Step 7 (reviewer protocol + audio_boundary extraction).
8. Step 8 (three new reviewers) — depends on 3, 4, 7.
9. Step 9 (refine orchestration) — depends on 2, 3, 6, 7, 8.
10. Step 10 (inspect_cut) — depends on 3, 9.
11. Step 11 (tests) — extended alongside each step; run as the final gate.

## Validation Order
1. **Cheap early**: `pytest tools/tests/test_enriched_arrangement.py tools/tests/test_quality_zones.py` after Steps 3 and 4.
2. **Schema surface**: `pytest tools/tests/test_arrangement_schema.py test_arrange.py test_multitrack_cut.py test_hype_cut_invariants.py` after Step 2.
3. **Biggest risk**: `pytest tools/tests/test_refine.py` after Step 9.
4. **Inspector**: `pytest tools/tests/test_inspect_cut.py` after Step 10.
5. **Full-suite regression diff** against Step 1 baseline: `pytest tools/tests -q` — *no new failing nodeids*.
6. **Manual smoke**: `python -m tools.pipeline --resume …` completes on the existing brief, and `python -m tools.inspect_cut runs/ados_pool/briefs/brief` renders SCRIPT + STRUCTURE + at least one `--clip` zoom.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-004",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 8 still makes draft creation happen only through the 800ms autosave path, while the publish flow immediately calls `syncembeds(postid, ...)` and `publishpost(postid, ...)`. if an author types content and clicks publish before the first autosave has created the row, there is no guaranteed `postid` for the primary publish path, so the plan has a runtime hole in the initial create-and-publish flow.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-005",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the cover fetch path is internally inconsistent. step 8 stores the selected cover on `posts.cover_media_id`, but step 5 says `usepost` fetches the post row plus `post_media` and `post_assets` and then 'resolves cover from `post.cover_media_id`'; because the cover picker is separate from body embeds and the plan never says to also link the chosen cover into `post_media`, a cover that is not embedded in the body would need its own direct media fetch or it will not be renderable.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-006",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the inline video plan does not fit the existing media pipeline. `mediauploader` only returns `file` objects, and the current upload flow writes a public supabase url into `cloudflare_thumbnail_url`; meanwhile every existing video surface (`src/pages/artdetail/index.tsx`, `src/pages/resourcedetail/index.tsx`, and `src/pages/resources/hlsplayer.tsx`) expects `cloudflare_playback_hls_url` for playable video, so uploaded videos would not render through `hlsplayer` as planned.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-029",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the proposed rpc auth path is internally inconsistent. `register_bundle_version(...)` is granted only to `service_role`, but its body authorizes with `auth.uid()` and `public.is_admin()`. in the current repo, edge functions authenticate the bearer separately with `supabase.auth.getuser(accesstoken)` and then run db work as service role (`supabase/functions/cloudflare-stream-ingest/index.ts:43-72`), so this sql function would not automatically see the caller's `auth.uid()` unless the plan adds a second authenticated client or rewrites the guard.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-030",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the storage/versioning sequence still has a race. step 3 uploads files under `post-bundles/bundles/<post_id>/<next_version>/...` before calling `register_bundle_version(...)`, but step 1 also makes the rpc compute `version = max(version) + 1` itself. concurrent uploads can therefore diverge between storage prefix and db version, and any rpc failure after upload leaves orphaned files because storage writes happened before the transactional sql step.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: compared step 3.11 in `plan_v6.md` with the vendored `tools/_reference/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/timelinerenderer.tsx:66-85`. the plan's scaled visual branch wraps a bare `<visualclip .../>` in `<absolutefill style={{transform: scale(...)}}>` but does not include a surrounding `<sequence from=... durationinframes=...>`. in the reference, that branch wraps `<visualclipsequence .../>`, which is where clip timing is applied, so implementing the plan literally would make scaled non-position-override clips mount from frame 0 for the full composition instead of honoring `clip.at` and `getclipdurationinframes(...)`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-of-a-4-sprint-plan-20260421-0520"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-058",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the proposed cache contract for the `cut` step is technically unsafe because it uses only `hype.timeline.json` as the sentinel even though `cut.py` writes `hype.timeline.json`, `hype.assets.json`, and `hype.metadata.json` as one unit (`tools/cut.py:763-769`). later planned steps consume the extra outputs as required inputs (`tools/render_remotion.py:62-99` requires `--assets`, and the bridge copies `hype.assets.json`), so a partial prior run or manual deletion of assets/metadata would still be treated as 'cached' and cause downstream breakage.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-final-of-a-4-sprint-20260421-0928"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-068",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: flagged that `--print-sql` still defaults `--timeline-id` to `<name>` even though `public.timelines.id` is a uuid column, so the default emitted `update` is not runnable as written.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-final-of-a-4-sprint-20260421-0928"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-069",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: flagged that byte-preserving `hype.assets.json` into `public.timelines` remains technically incomplete because reigh-app resolves asset `file` values as http urls or `timeline-assets` bucket keys, while `cut.py` writes local absolute paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-final-of-a-4-sprint-20260421-0928"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "audio-playback": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-054",

  "bundle-registration-auth": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "bundle registration auth: `register_bundle_version(...)` is granted only to `service_role` but authorizes with `auth.uid()`/`public.is_admin()` internally, which does not match the repo's existing edge function pattern where service-role clients validate the bearer separately before doing db work.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "bundle-versioning": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "bundle versioning: step 3 uploads extracted files into a `<next_version>` storage prefix before the database allocates a version, but step 1 also computes `version = max(version) + 1` inside the rpc; concurrent uploads can mismatch storage and db state, and failed rpcs leave orphaned bundle files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the actual migration at `/users/user_c042661f/documents/banodoco-workspace/supabase/migrations/20260325070000_create_posts.sql`. the repository already defines `posts`, `post_media`, and `post_assets`, with `posts.body`, `posts.slug`, `posts.status` allowing `'archived'`, and ownership/rls keyed through `members.auth_user_id`; the plan still treats that schema as unreadable and proposes a materially different surface (`body_md`, `author_user_id`, draft/published-only status), which is not the minimal extension the brief asked for.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the brief says to reuse the existing join tables, but the live schema is `post_media(post_id, media_id, sort_order, caption)` and `post_assets(post_id, asset_id)`. the plan instead assumes generic `position` columns and ownership checks built around `profiles.discord_id`, so it is not aligned with the repository's current posts infrastructure.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-016",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still only writes `post_media` / `post_assets` rows during the explicit publish action via `syncembeds`, even though the brief also requires editing existing posts and step 8 says edit-mode saves preserve the current status. that leaves the repository's existing join-table requirement only partially covered for ordinary edits, because a published post can be saved with a new body before its join rows are updated.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-a-user-authored-posts-20260420-1310"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-027",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still treats markdown-mode ownership of `/submit/post` and `/posts/:slug` as parallel-branch work, but those surfaces already exist in this repo: `src/app.tsx:42-53` already wires the routes and `src/pages/submitpost/index.tsx:59-380` already owns the markdown draft/publish flow. bundle mode therefore needs an explicit integration story for the existing editor now, not merge-later language.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-028",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the `admin_status` fix is still only partial. step 4 hides bundle bytes for `admin_status='hidden'`, but the surrounding post page is still expected to load through `usepost`, and `src/hooks/usepost.ts:223-229` plus the live posts rls in `supabase/migrations/20260325070000_create_posts.sql:21-24` still allow any published post row to be read. a hidden bundle post could therefore still leak title/body/post chrome even if the iframe itself 404s.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-the-bundle-mode-posts-20260420-2034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-048",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: deliverable 3 says the python wrapper may pass props inline or via a props file, but step 4.2 writes a `.remotion-props.json` file and step 4.3 still invokes `npx remotion render ... --props props_path`. that is an internal contract mismatch in the approved plan itself: the command is treating a filename like an inline props argument, so the wrapper path is still only partially specified against the requested cli behavior.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-of-a-4-sprint-plan-20260421-0520"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the actual reigh-app integration path and found that the editor is not file-backed. `usetimelineslist()` lists and creates rows in `public.timelines` with `project_id`, `user_id`, default `config`, and default `asset_registry` (`reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelineslist.ts:11-55`), while `supabasedataprovider` only loads and saves `config` and `asset_registry` from that table (`reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/data/supabasedataprovider.ts:51-109,192-204`; schema in `reigh-app/supabase/migrations/20260325090000_create_video_editor_tables.sql:1-27`). the plan's `open_in_reigh.py` copy-to-`public/timelines` or `public/demos` bridge does not match that storage model, so against the current repository it would produce inert files rather than something the editor can open.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-final-of-a-4-sprint-20260421-0928"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-057",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: i ran the plan's stated regression command, `python3 -m unittest discover tools/tests`, from the actual git root `/users/user_c042661f/documents/banodoco-workspace`, and it failed with `modulenotfounderror` for `cut`, `render_remotion`, and `timeline`. that happens because the checked-in tests import those modules directly from `tools/` (`tools/tests/test_cut_timeline_resume.py:7`, `tools/tests/test_audio_render.py:7-8`), so the validation section does not currently line up with the repository's executable test layout.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]

      "occurrence_count": 1,

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: i searched every current detail/profile routing integration point. the plan names `src/app.tsx` and `src/lib/routing.ts`, but it does not call out `src/layouts/mainlayout.tsx` and `src/components/layout/header.tsx`, both of which hard-code resource-context path checks and would need `/posts` awareness for theme selection and the header logo target to stay consistent with the rest of the 2rp-style surfaces.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the repository also has legacy username repair in `normalizelegacyhashusernamepath()` that only recognizes `/art` and `/resources`. adding `/:username/posts` without extending that helper leaves one of the existing routing safety nets incomplete for post urls.",

'resources' : 'art'`. the plan says to add a posts branch later in the file, but it does not explicitly cover this earlier tab-selection logic, so `/:username/posts` would still resolve to the wrong active tab unless that code is updated too.",

      "id": "DEBT-017",

      "occurrence_count": 1,

      "id": "DEBT-032",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the route inventory has moved since the earlier critique. `src/app.tsx:42-53` already contains `/posts/:slug`, `/:username/posts/:slug`, and `/:username/posts`, but both detail routes still point at `notfound`, so the required work here is replacing an existing placeholder surface and integrating admin routing, not simply adding fresh routes.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan's hard-coded `frame-ancestors https://banodoco.com https://www.banodoco.com` is not enough supporting infrastructure for this repo as written. the validation section explicitly expects staging verification, but a fixed production-only ancestor list would prevent embedding from local or staging hosts and there is no existing site-origin constant in the checked-in frontend to keep those values in sync.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-047",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: supporting infrastructure miss: the post-ship refetch path. the plan's step 13.3 acknowledges the flow ('triggers a post refetch so isexistingbundlepost auto-switches') but the referenced `refreshpost()` helper doesn't exist in src/hooks/usepost.ts. this is a supporting-infrastructure gap: either usepost needs a new `refetch` export (one small edit to a shipped file) or handlevibeshipped needs a different mechanism. plan should name the exact change to src/hooks/usepost.ts if option (a) is chosen.",

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the remaining timing problem is not isolated to one sentence in step 3.11. `plan_v6.meta.json` still treats the scaled-track behavior as verified if `hypecomposition.tsx` contains the `scale(track.scale)` wrapper, but no success criterion checks that the scaled branch also preserves `sequence from/durationinframes`. with the current plan package, an implementation could satisfy the wrapper-only criterion and still schedule scaled clips incorrectly for the full composition.",
